------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every way load_data() can refuse
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> load_data()
  [ok] refused: 'assays' must be supplied.

> load_data(1)
  [ok] refused: 'assays' must be a list.

> load_data(list())
  [ok] refused: 'assays' is empty.

> load_data(list(a = NULL))
  [ok] refused: No valid data blocks were supplied.

> load_data(list(a = 1))
  [ok] refused: Each assay must be either a matrix or a data.frame.

> load_data(list(data.frame(x = 1)))
  [ok] refused: 'assays' must be a named list.

> load_data(list(a = data.frame(x = integer(0))))
  [ok] refused: Block 'a' contains no samples.

> load_data(list(a = data.frame(row.names = 1:5)))
  [ok] refused: Block 'a' contains no variables.

  A matrix with no row names is refused on purpose:

> load_data(list(a = matrix(rnorm(20), nrow = 5)))
  [ok] refused: Block 'a' has no sample identifiers. Set them before calling load_data(), e.g. rownames(x) <- ids.

  Duplicated names, in both directions:

> load_data(list(bad = dup_cols))
  [ok] refused: Duplicated feature names detected in 'bad'.

> load_data(list(bad = dup_rows))
  [ok] refused: Duplicated sample identifiers detected in 'bad'.

  Modality has to name blocks that exist:

> load_data(list(a = STUDY$assays[[1]]), modality = c(nope = "rnaseq"))
  [ok] refused: 'modality' names blocks that are not in 'assays': nope.

==============================================================================
SECTION 7   analyze(): building evidence
==============================================================================

  ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
  What analyze() is for, and what it refuses to be
  ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

  Most analysis functions fit a model and hand it back. This one does not,
  because 'the best model' is a bad answer to a biological question: the
  model that predicts best is usually the one that leans hardest on
  whatever was measured most accurately, which is a fact about the
  laboratory rather than about the disease.

  Instead every applicable method runs, each reports the relationships it
  found, and an integrator merges them into one directed graph in which
  every arrow carries its own evidence: which methods saw it, how large it
  was, how precisely it was measured, and - the part that matters - what
  would have to be true for it to mean what it looks like it means.

  A relationship between two variables can arise three ways. One causes the
  other. Something else causes both. Or the sampling created it. No amount
  of statistics distinguishes these from a single cross-section, so the
  engine never claims to; it records which of the three it has ruled out
  and leaves the rest visible.


------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Run it
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The blocks are named explicitly. NHANES gave its modules to different
  subsamples of people, so requiring everyone to have everything leaves
  nobody - a later section watches the package explain exactly that. Here
  the set is the largest one that still leaves a real population.

  The choice is made on the preprocessed blocks, not the raw ones, and the
  difference is large. Before preprocessing, a person with one of eight
  hormone measurements looks like someone who was measured. After it, the
  filter that drops mostly-empty rows has removed them, and the hormone
  block has gone from 1800 people to 413. Choosing blocks on the raw counts
  would pick a set that cannot be analysed.


  blocks analysed: blood, biochemistry, body_measures, blood_pressure, nutrition, disease_history

> analyze(clean, blocks = ANALYSIS_BLOCKS, outcome = STUDY$outcome, 
      covariates = STUDY$covariates, plots = TRUE, quiet = FALSE)

Top paths to the outcome
------------------------
None

Limitations
-----------
- Observational data: no relationship reported here is identified by design.
- 0 of 181 relationships carry temporal precedence.
- Agreement between methods measures stability, not validity: methods sharing an unmeasured confounder agree while all being biased.
- Analysis rests on 687 sample(s) shared by every analysed block; 1113 were dropped by the intersection.
- The graph was not calibrated against a null. Without that there is no way to know how many of these relationships the engine would find on data with no signal at all; run with permutations > 0.
- Under 50 bootstrap resamples, 86 of 181 relationships were recovered less than half the time.
- 11 of 161 features were screened out against the outcome before the pairwise stage and were never examined for indirect roles.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The analysis report, on screen
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> report(results, format = "console")

==============================================================================
CausalMultiOmics - Analysis Report
==============================================================================

  Outcome              died
  Design               cross-sectional
  Samples              687
  Relationships        181

What was found
--------------
  - any_ht shows the strongest evidence for a relationship with died (score
    27.3, 4 method(s), adjusted association).
  - 0 of 181 integrated relationships carry temporal precedence; the rest are
    adjusted associations and cannot rule out confounding.

Strongest relationships
-----------------------
  Higher any_ht goes together with higher died          27.3  Other factors accounted for
  Higher DR1TS120 goes together with lower died         17.5  Other factors accounted for
  Higher LBXHCY goes together with higher died          13.7  Other factors accounted for
  Higher DR1TFIBE goes together with lower died         12.6  Other factors accounted for
  Higher LBXSTP goes together with higher died          13.1  Other factors accounted for
  Higher LBXBAP goes together with higher died          11.5  Other factors accounted for
  Higher DR1TCALC goes together with lower died         11.5  Other factors accounted for
  Higher DR1TVB1 goes together with lower died           9.7  Other factors accounted for
  Higher any_diabetes goes together with higher died     9.6  Other factors accounted for
  Higher DR1TMAGN goes together with lower died          9.6  Other factors accounted for

Limitations
-----------
  - Observational data: no relationship reported here is identified by
    design.
  - 0 of 181 relationships carry temporal precedence.
  - Agreement between methods measures stability, not validity: methods
    sharing an unmeasured confounder agree while all being biased.
  - Analysis rests on 687 sample(s) shared by every analysed block; 1113 were
    dropped by the intersection.
  - The graph was not calibrated against a null. Without that there is no way
    to know how many of these relationships the engine would find on data
    with no signal at all; run with permutations > 0.
  - Under 50 bootstrap resamples, 86 of 181 relationships were recovered less
    than half the time.
  - 11 of 161 features were screened out against the outcome before the
    pairwise stage and were never examined for indirect roles.

==============================================================================
SECTION 12  The effort dial: buying rigour with time
==============================================================================

  Everything expensive is opt-in through one argument, with each piece separately overridable. Resampling and permutation are the only parts that cost real time, and both scale linearly.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
effort = 'fast': the generators once, nothing else
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> analyze(clean, blocks = ANALYSIS_BLOCKS, STUDY$outcome, effort = "fast", 
      plots = FALSE, quiet = TRUE)

CMOResult
=========

Design:                        cross-sectional
Samples:                       687
Features analysed:             150 of 161

Methods run:                   7
Relationships found:           403
After integration:             200
With temporal precedence:      0

Causal paths:                  0
Plots:                         0
Tables:                        15
Runtime:                       8.23 s

  elapsed: 8.2 s

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
effort = 'standard': adds model diagnostics and 50 resamples
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> analyze(clean, blocks = ANALYSIS_BLOCKS, STUDY$outcome, effort = "standard", 
      plots = FALSE, quiet = TRUE)

CMOResult
=========

Outcome:                       died

Samples:                       687
Features analysed:             150 of 161

Methods run:                   7
Relationships found:           403
After integration:             200
With temporal precedence:      0

Causal paths:                  0
Plots:                         0
Tables:                        15
Runtime:                       40.57 s

  elapsed: 40.6 s

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
What each preset turns on
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  fast       resampling: FALSE  null calibration: FALSE  diagnostics: FALSE

  standard   resampling: TRUE   null calibration: FALSE  diagnostics: TRUE

  'thorough' adds null calibration; 'exhaustive' resamples every generator 500 times. Any component can be set on its own: resample =, permutations =, diagnostics =.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Overriding a single piece
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> analyze(clean, blocks = ANALYSIS_BLOCKS, STUDY$outcome, effort = "fast", 
      resample = 20, plots = FALSE, quiet = TRUE)

==============================================================================
SECTION 13  Opening up an evidence score
==============================================================================

  Three numbers, kept apart on purpose. Size is how big the relationship is, Precision how tightly it was estimated, and Agreement how many methods saw the same thing.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The evidence hierarchy: not every method makes the same kind of claim
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  association      level 2  associational  weight 1.0
  conditional      level 2  associational  weight 1.0
  survival         level 3  temporal       weight 2.0
  longitudinal     level 3  temporal       weight 2.0
  mediation        level 4  mechanistic    weight 2.5
  elasticnet       level 1  predictive     weight 0.4
  randomforest     level 1  predictive     weight 0.4
  bayesnet         level 2  associational  weight 1.0
  sem              level 2  associational  weight 1.0

  Agreement is weighted by this. Five predictive methods concurring is weaker evidence than one longitudinal model plus one mediation analysis, and an unweighted vote says the opposite.

  What each method found on its own
                           method               measured pooled estimate CI low CI high        p   n agrees
              logistic regression         log odds ratio   TRUE  0.74648  0.312    1.18 0.000762 687   TRUE
                      elastic net  penalised coefficient  FALSE  0.49266     NA      NA       NA 687   TRUE

  Headline estimate: log odds ratio, pooled from 1 observation(s).
  Not combined, being different measurements: penalised coefficient, permutation importance, network arc strength

  What those methods are

    association
      Fits a straight line between the variable and the outcome,
      after subtracting the effect of any factors you asked it to
      account for. 
      Limitation: It cannot tell which one came first, and it can
      only account for factors that were actually measured. 

    elasticnet
      Fits every variable at once and shrinks the useless ones to
      exactly zero, so what survives is a short list that predicts
      the outcome together. 
      Limitation: When two variables carry the same information it
      keeps one and drops the other, so being dropped is not evidence
      of having no effect. 

    randomforest
      Builds hundreds of decision trees and measures how much worse
      the predictions get when one variable is scrambled. A big drop
      means the variable carried real information. 
      Limitation: It measures usefulness for prediction, not the size
      or the direction of an effect. Something downstream of the
      outcome predicts it beautifully. 

    bayesnet
      Searches for a network of arrows that explains the observed
      pattern of correlations with as few connections as possible. 
      Limitation: Several different arrangements of arrows explain
      the same data equally well, so the direction it picks is often
      one of many that fit. 

  Score, opened up
                component      value weight                                                     note
              Effect size 0.42479296   0.34                            how large the relationship is
                Precision 0.79399415   0.33                             how tightly it was estimated
         Method agreement 1.00000000   0.33           weighted by the epistemic level of each method
             Data quality 0.81005556     NA        scales the score; limited by ever_osteo_arthritis
 Spread across the cohort 0.02183406     NA                          halved by removing 15 sample(s)
           Evidence level 0.40000000     NA                     highest level reached: associational
         Temporal support 0.00000000     NA                                     no measurement order
     Direction confidence         NA     NA                                                 untested
      Bootstrap stability 1.00000000     NA                              recovered in 1 of resamples
    Sensitivity (E-value) 0.72789721     NA E = 3.64, explaining this away needs a strong confounder
       Biological support         NA     NA               reported alongside, never inside the score

  What would have to be true
    - Conditioned on: age, female. 
    - Causal only if that set blocks every backdoor path, which the
      data cannot verify. 
    - No unmeasured confounding; no adjustment for a collider or a
      mediator. 
    - Penalised multivariable fit: every selected feature is
      conditioned on the others. 
    - Selection is not inference; the penalty biases coefficients
      towards zero. 
    - Correlated features compete, so an omitted feature is not
      evidence of no effect. 
    - Permutation importance is a predictive quantity, not an effect
      estimate. 
    - Importance is conditional on the other features present in the
      forest. 
    - Correlated features share importance, which dilutes both. 
    - Structure learned from observational data: the DAG is identified
      only up to its Markov equivalence class. 
    - Arc directions within an equivalence class are not empirically
      distinguishable. 
    - Causal sufficiency: no unmeasured common cause of any two nodes. 
    - Linear Gaussian dependence. 

Ranked by 4 method(s), combined ranking 0.868

SECTION 15  counterfactual(): what changing something would mean
==============================================================================

  Expressed in the units the variable was measured in. The engine pushes the original value through the stored preprocessing models to locate it on the scale the analysis worked in.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Only variables you declare as changeable are considered
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> counterfactual(results)
  [ok] refused: 'modifiable' must name the variables or blocks you could plausibly act on.

  Nothing is included by default on purpose: a contrast for a genotype is arithmetic without meaning, and printing one invites exactly the reading it cannot support.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Requiring stronger identification empties the table rather than relaxing
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> counterfactual(results, modifiable = STUDY$modifiable[1], min_identification = "temporal")

  rows: 0
  reason: DXDTOBMD (identification: adjustment); BMXCALF (identification: adjustment); BMXSUB (identification: adjustment); DXXHEBMD (identification: adjustment); BMXHT (identification: adjustment); BMXBMI (identification: adjustment)

  The verb is chosen by the identification label, never freely. An adjusted association reads 'is associated with', however high its score.


==============================================================================
SECTION 16  Factors, binary and with several categories
==============================================================================

  Factors survive loading and preprocessing untouched, which is right: nothing sensible comes of scaling a category. They are encoded at the analysis step.

> load_data(list(blood = data.frame(MARKER = marker, OTHER = rnorm(m), 
      row.names = ids), lifestyle = data.frame(sex = sex, diet = diet, 
      barcode = paste0("id_", seq_len(m)), row.names = ids, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)), 
      metadata = data.frame(sample_id = ids, score = y, stringsAsFactors = FALSE))

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Factors are still factors after preprocessing
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> vapply(cat_prep$data$assays$lifestyle, class, character(1))
        sex        diet     barcode 
"character" "character" "character" 

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
analyze() encodes them
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> analyze(cat_prep, "score", effort = "fast", plots = FALSE, quiet = TRUE)
  sex=M                M vs F   (58 vs 62 people)
  diet=western         western vs mediterranean   (43 vs 50 people)
  diet=vegetarian      vegetarian vs mediterranean   (27 vs 50 people)

  A factor of k levels becomes k-1 comparisons against a reference, and the reference is the commonest level, because contrasts against a rare category are estimated from very few people.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
What was left out, and why
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> grep("categor|identifier|fewer than", cat_res$logs, value = TRUE)
[1] "[lifestyle] 'barcode' has 120 categories, which is more than 10: treated as an identifier and left out."

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The wording changes for a category
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> cat_res$report$main_findings
[1] "sex=M shows the strongest evidence for a relationship with score (score 41.3, 5 method(s), adjusted association)."             
[2] "diet=vegetarian appears as a mediator on the highest-scoring paths."                                                           
[3] "0 of 6 integrated relationships carry temporal precedence; the rest are adjusted associations and cannot rule out confounding."

An unordered outcome with more than two categories is refused
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  [ok] refused: Outcome 'sex_is_not_here' is not a column of the metadata.


==============================================================================
SECTION 18  How much to trust any of this
==============================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Would these relationships survive a different sample?
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  50 bootstrap replicates over: association, conditional, survival, longitudinal
  recovered every time : 75
  recovered under half : 85

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
How many relationships appear when there is nothing to find
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> analyze(clean, blocks = ANALYSIS_BLOCKS, STUDY$outcome, effort = "fast", 
      permutations = 15, plots = FALSE, quiet = TRUE)

  found here          : 115
  found on shuffled   : 101.8 on average, up to 118
  empirical p         : 0.188

  The number of relationships found is not clearly more than chance produces.

  The single most useful number about a graph. Twenty-five edges sounds like a result until the same engine produces twenty-two on the same data with the outcome shuffled.

  Not combined, being different measurements: penalised coefficient, permutation importance, network arc strength

  What those methods are

    association
      Fits a straight line between the variable and the outcome,
      after subtracting the effect of any factors you asked it to
      account for. 
      Limitation: It cannot tell which one came first, and it can
      only account for factors that were actually measured. 

    elasticnet
      Fits every variable at once and shrinks the useless ones to
      exactly zero, so what survives is a short list that predicts
      the outcome together. 
      Limitation: When two variables carry the same information it
      keeps one and drops the other, so being dropped is not evidence
      of having no effect. 

    randomforest
      Builds hundreds of decision trees and measures how much worse
      the predictions get when one variable is scrambled. A big drop
      means the variable carried real information. 
      Limitation: It measures usefulness for prediction, not the size
      or the direction of an effect. Something downstream of the
      outcome predicts it beautifully. 

    bayesnet
      Searches for a network of arrows that explains the observed
      pattern of correlations with as few connections as possible. 
      Limitation: Several different arrangements of arrows explain
      the same data equally well, so the direction it picks is often
      one of many that fit.

 E = 3.64, explaining this away needs a strong confounder

  What would have to be true
    - Conditioned on: age, female. 
    - Causal only if that set blocks every backdoor path, which the
      data cannot verify. 
    - No unmeasured confounding; no adjustment for a collider or a
      mediator. 
    - Penalised multivariable fit: every selected feature is
      conditioned on the others. 
    - Selection is not inference; the penalty biases coefficients
      towards zero. 
    - Correlated features compete, so an omitted feature is not
      evidence of no effect. 
    - Permutation importance is a predictive quantity, not an effect
      estimate. 
    - Importance is conditional on the other features present in the
      forest. 
    - Correlated features share importance, which dilutes both. 
    - Structure learned from observational data: the DAG is identified
      only up to its Markov equivalence class. 
    - Arc directions within an equivalence class are not empirically
      distinguishable. 
    - Causal sufficiency: no unmeasured common cause of any two nodes. 
    - Linear Gaussian dependence.

Hypothesis
==========================================================================

  Higher any_ht goes with higher died.

  This is an adjusted association carried by a small minority.

  Effect:                0.7465 (log odds ratio)
  95% CI:                [0.3119, 1.1811]
  Identification:        adjustment

  - Measured confounders were adjusted for; unmeasured ones cannot be.
  - Removing 15 people halves it.

What supports it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  + 4 independent methods found it, agreeing on the direction: logistic
    regression, elastic net, random forest importance, Bayesian network
    (hill climbing).
  + It survives correction for the number of relationships tested (FDR
    0.0122).
  + Explaining it away needs a confounder associated with both at 3.6 or
    more, which is stronger than most measured risk factors.
  + It reappeared in 100% of resamples of this cohort.

What threatens it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Part of it rests on values that were filled in rather than measured
    (quality 0.81).

What would settle it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  > Measure a confounder of any_ht and died. To erase this it would have
    to be associated with both at a risk ratio of at least 3.6; anything
    weaker leaves the relationship standing.
  > Measure any_ht before died in the same people. Nothing in this data
    establishes which came first, and the reverse direction fits it
    equally well.
  > Replicate in a cohort collected independently. Here the estimate is
    halved by removing 15 people, so it may describe them rather than the
    population.
  > Measure ever_osteo_arthritis in the people whose value was filled in.
    Part of this estimate rests on numbers that were reconstructed rather
    than observed.

  From CausalMultiOmics 0.1.0, 687 sample(s), score 27.3.


Hypothesis
==========================================================================

  Higher DR1TS120 goes with lower died.

  This is an adjusted association carried by a small minority.

  Effect:                -0.3174 (log odds ratio)
  95% CI:                [-0.5814, -0.0535]
  Identification:        adjustment

  - Measured confounders were adjusted for; unmeasured ones cannot be.
  - Removing 10 people halves it.

What supports it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  + Explaining it away needs a confounder associated with both at 2.1 or
    more, which is stronger than most measured risk factors.
  + It reappeared in 100% of resamples of this cohort.

What threatens it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Only one method found it (logistic regression), so nothing
    corroborates it.
  - It does not survive correction for multiplicity (FDR 0.106).
  - Part of it rests on values that were filled in rather than measured
    (quality 0.99).

What would settle it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  > Measure a confounder of DR1TS120 and died. To erase this it would
    have to be associated with both at a risk ratio of at least 2.1;
    anything weaker leaves the relationship standing.
  > Measure DR1TS120 before died in the same people. Nothing in this data
    establishes which came first, and the reverse direction fits it
    equally well.
  > Replicate in a cohort collected independently. Here the estimate is
    halved by removing 10 people, so it may describe them rather than the
    population.
  > Measure DR1TS120 in the people whose value was filled in. Part of
    this estimate rests on numbers that were reconstructed rather than
    observed.
  > Separate DR1TS120 from the 5 other variables it moves with. In this
    data they cannot be told apart, so the effect may belong to any of
    them or to whatever drives all of them.

  From CausalMultiOmics 0.1.0, 687 sample(s), score 17.5.
